- 2026-09-23 08:05 [engineer] SYMMETRIC-BILLING MEASUREMENT on S6b+coldbind3 (no builds; sym_census.py, sym_price.py, sym_time.py, sym_server_check*.py, sym_elem_census.py in fable_engineer_v1). (1) Symmetric-output products = 5.4% of the bill (r35 cold / r100 warm alike): covariance second product w^T(Cw) 4.3% (14/net; symmetric output, NOT a Gram), old-tier joined core Fj diag(w) Fj^T 0.78% (3/net, 384 x 4.6-6.1k x 384, weighted Gram), layer-0 W^T W 0.24% (_resident_gram512 already computes 3 of 4 blocks), old core sandwich 0.06%. Everything else is non-symmetric: star + C w + w^T A 17.2% (square), pushes/reads 68% (rect). No (P o P) L (P o P)^T read exists in S6b. Elementwise ops with a symmetric square operand: 0.22% of the bill (half-price SymmetricTensor ops would save <= 0.1%). (2) Prices, units of 2mkn, server path (bill identical in-process): covariance second product 0.551 -> 0.435 with one level of upper blocks (deeper is worse: 256-blocks run dense), at +100 ms server CPU each; Cholesky route (chol 0.167 + L^T w 0.551 + Gram 0.396-0.5) = 1.11-1.22 vs 1.10 now: no gain, and C is singular when units die. Weighted Gram 0.591 -> 0.503 as einsum('ia,a,ja->ij'), 240 -> 30 ms. Layer-0 Gram 0.435 -> 0.396 with einsum diagonal blocks, 243 -> 103 ms. (3) Server path: discount holds; symmetric einsum is BLAS-backed (30 ms vs 17 ms plain GEMM vs 130-240 ms for our Winograd paths); S+S on a SymmetricTensor bills 0.5/element, astype drops the symmetry. SAVINGS: covariance upper blocks -0.91% bill at +1.4 s server CPU/net (2.5 Gflop per CPU-second, ~4x pad07's rate); weighted-Gram einsum -0.13% at -0.63 s; layer-0 -0.02% at -0.14 s. All three: about -1.05% bill at +0.6 s/net; the two win-win items alone -0.15% at -0.77 s. Not bit-exact (rounding level; einsum is the more accurate path).

- 2026-09-25 11:30 [engineer2] RANK-CUT MECHANISM (lead's question; code: stack_S27s _slr_reconstruct / lazy _cp_slr_inner_sq): S27's old-tier joins are LAZY. After a join the pre-join columns (prefix) are rebuilt and read against the base factors at the ARRIVING rank (prefix = (U map) base, inner0 at r_base, then mapped to r_new); only the new cohort (recent = U join), the old-basis push and part of the join run at the new rank, and the NEXT join materializes the prefix at the new rank. So 320 at L13 alone leaves the 11,776 pre-L13 columns at 384 through L13-15: about 12.05 U x 28% cohort x 16.7% + push/join = about 0.9 U = -0.65% (measured -0.688%). 320 at L10 also converts all of L13-15 (materialized at the L13 join), which is the additive -2.48%. A cut at the LAST join buys little; value arrives one join later. Materializing at the join itself costs about r_new x r_base x prefix per join (+0.7 U at L10 vs -0.9 U saved at L10-12): not worth it. Exact per-component deltas (S27s/S27r vs S27, net 100) are queued behind the machine gate (bill27/run_census_rank.sh -> rank_delta_S27r/s.txt).
- 2026-09-25 11:30 lead: WHY THE L13 CUT SAVES LITTLE (engineer2, from the code): the joins are LAZY. After a join, the pre-join columns keep the ARRIVING tier's rank (base factors at r_base, plus the new cohort at r_new, plus an r_new x r_base map) until the next join materializes them. So a cut at join k shrinks its own cohort in segment k AND the whole prefix in segment k+1. L13 cut: only the 4.6k-column L13 cohort at L13-15, about -0.65% (measured -0.688%). L10 cut: the L10-12 cohort plus the 11.8k-column prefix at L13-15, about -2.5% (the additive -2.48%). Early materialization at the new rank costs more than it saves (+0.7 U vs -0.9 U at L10), so the lazy design stands.
  Consequence: the L7 cut also owns the L10-12 prefix, so its native bill saving is likely well above the model's -1.03%. The porter's native bill map (8 schedules) and the analyst's lazy-aware table decide S28's schedule. Candidates: 384/320/384 (S27t, building), 320/384/384, 352/320/384, 320/320/384.

- 2026-09-25 13:15 [engineer2] SPLIT u/v OLD TIER, NATIVE BILL, STEP 1 (lead 13:0x; bill prototype, no accuracy claim; fable_engineer2_v1/uvsplit/uvp_fragment_v0.py c247f245 on S28's pre-treuse file + treuse; in process, setup first). Design = NESTED frame: one basis, pair means F_u kept in the frame's leading r_u = 256 coordinates, half differences F_v/2 in all of them, so the basis push and the trailing read stay at rank 384. (Two separate frames would push two bases per layer and read twice at the end: those products run at (256 + 384)/384 = 1.67x, about +0.9% of the bill.) Step 1 splits only the REBUILD (U F) and the READ ((L^2 lam) F^T), for the materialized frame and both lazy-L13 blocks. The joins are unchanged, except that the Nystrom rows now go in energy order, so the QR frame's leading coordinates carry the most energy.
  Bill vs S28: net 35 -1.343%, net 100 -1.613%. Net 100 breakdown: rebuild 33.5 -> 30.0 GF (-10.4% of the function, -1.18% of the bill); read 20.1 -> 18.8 GF (-6.5%, -0.44%); everything else unchanged. The ideal (256+384)/768 = -16.7% is not reached because the Winograd transforms do not shrink: the rebuild transforms U twice (U[:, :256] and U) and pays a pair recombination, and the read's k-heavy products keep their A-side transform, grow the output side, and add ~3 elementwise passes over rows x columns.
  Accuracy glance (no claim): final MSE +0.41% (35), +1.43% (100); per-layer max|diff|/RMS <= 5.6e-5. The u truncation here uses the energy-ordered frame, not u's own principal frame.
  STEP 2 now: split the JOIN (S27 census: joins ~6% of the bill): Q_u from the u columns' own Nystrom range (rank 256), then the v residual's 128 directions, Qn = [Q_u | Q_c]; Fj, core and overlap in u/v form. Step 2 also makes the u frame u-optimal.
